====================================================================================================
QUESTION: What happens when the first insured dies?
CATEGORY: CAT3_CONDITIONAL_REASONING
EXPECTED ANSWER: No death benefit is paid. The policy continues in force, premium payment continues, the policy owner receives written confirmation, and a death certificate must be filed.
EVIDENCE: 3.1 When the first insured dies, no death benefit is paid, but: (a) Policy continues in force (b) Premium payment obligation continues (c) Policy owner receives written confirmation (d) Death certificate must be filed with us
====================================================================================================

--- GOAL PLAN ---
rewritten_query: What happens when the first insured person dies in a joint life insurance policy?
reasoning: The query "What happens when the first insured dies?" is asking about the consequences or outcomes in a specific contractual or financial context—likely related to a joint life insurance policy, a survivorship policy, or a similar arrangement where two or more individuals are insured together. The key here is understanding the standard legal or financial mechanism that triggers when the first insured person passes away.

This is a single, well-defined factual question about a known insurance policy mechanism. It does not require chaining through multiple independent facts—there is no ambiguity about what needs to be looked up. The answer is a standard clause in life insurance contracts: typically, the death benefit is paid out upon the first death in a joint policy, and the policy may terminate or continue depending on the type (e.g., second-to-die policies).

No intermediate facts need to be discovered before answering this. The query is self-contained and can be answered with one lookup: the standard outcome of the first death in a joint life insurance policy.
